BONUS DIFETTO se mai cablato: detect_mandate_nine_tag_mismatch_blocking solleva GateError(44, ...), ma il codice 44 e' gia' quello dedicato a CARPACCIO_SLICE_TOO_LARGE (usato ~7 volte in carpaccio_format.py, documentato nella tabella exit-code in cima allo stesso modulo) -- cablare questa funzione cosi' com'e' collasserebbe due classi di fallimento semanticamente distinte sullo stesso exit code. Viola il mandato 'catalogued != wired' di CLAUDE.md." proposed_solution="O (a) cablare un harvester AST-based di composition-evidence + chiamare detect_mandate_nine_tag_mismatch_blocking(blocking_mode=True, ...) da main() per ogni scenario prima di ritornare SliceCleared, assegnando un exit code distinto (non 44), oppure (b) se la feature e' deliberatamente differita, correggere Status: SHIPPED nel documento di evoluzione e rimuovere l'affermazione di 'applicazione meccanica' finche' non e' cablata." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 via option (b), deliberately -- option (a) is out of scope for a mechanical techdebt-drain fix: it requires designing and building the composition_evidence AST harvester DD-1 describes (net-new production code, plus resolving the GateError(44) exit-code collision this row itself flags), which is exactly the kind of judgment call the orchestrator asked me to use discretion on rather than force through. Re-verified every claim empirically before editing: `carpaccio_slice_gate.main()` (src/des/cli/carpaccio_slice_gate.py:869-950) calls only check_carpaccio/check_at_review; grepped src/des/ and scripts/ for detect_mandate_nine_tag_mismatch(_blocking)/read_slice_kinds_from_catalog -- zero production callers, only tests/des/acceptance/fix_mandate_9_v2_rollout/conftest.py. Added a dated correction block to docs/evolution/2026-05-27-fix-mandate-9-v2-rollout.md's Status line and top section, naming the unwired symbols, their sole test-only callers, and the GateError(44) collision explicitly, and pointing at this techdebt row for the wire-or-retire decision -- so the document no longer overclaims mechanical enforcement that does not exist. No code changed (detector/tests untouched, no regression risk); grepped for any gate/script reading this doc's Status field -- none found. Deferred: whether to actually wire it (option a) is a product decision for Ale, not mine to make unilaterally under a techdebt-drain mandate."
- [x] session-start-handler-multiple-uncoordinated-prints-corrupt-stdout-json: paradigm=object-oriented defect="handle_session_start() (src/des/adapters/drivers/hooks/session_start_handler.py:750-880) spara fino a 6 print() indipendenti e non coordinati (righe 341,797,827,835,845,868), ognuno stampando il proprio oggetto JSON, invece di un unico payload strutturato -- violando sia il proprio docstring di modulo (righe 10-21, che dichiara un SINGOLO payload hookSpecificOutput/systemMessage) sia il pattern gia' seguito da OGNI handler fratello (pre_tool_use_handler.py, post_tool_use_handler.py), che costruiscono un solo dict response e chiamano print(json.dumps(response)) una sola volta. RIPRODOTTO DAL VIVO: progetto sintetico con workflow.mode non dichiarato + un update disponibile + una feature-delta attiva -> stdout catturato contiene TRE oggetti JSON separati su tre righe; json.loads() sull'intero output solleva json.JSONDecodeError: Extra data. Un consumatore che si aspetta UN oggetto o legge solo la prima riga (scartando le altre in silenzio) o fallisce il parsing dell'intero output. NESSUNO dei 6 file di test che asseriscono sullo stdout di questo handler esercita due trigger contemporaneamente -- assumono tutti implicitamente un solo oggetto. Costo: nel caso realistico e ORDINARIO di un progetto con mode non dichiarato + update pendente + feature attiva, al massimo UNO fra {guida workflow, notifica update, nudge gate-affordance, warning hook-version-skew, orchestrator affordance} raggiunge il modello per sessione; gli altri vengono persi in silenzio -- vanificando la garanzia dichiarata dal rilevatore di skew stesso ('the PRIMARY hook-version skew detector... mechanically fired every session')." proposed_solution="Sostituire le 6 print() indipendenti con UN accumulatore (lista di stringhe additionalContext + systemMessage opzionale) attraversato per tutto il corpo della funzione; ogni trigger appende all'accumulatore nel proprio try/except invece di stampare; emettere UN solo print(json.dumps(...)) alla fine, unendo i contributi multipli di additionalContext con '\\n\\n' (precedente gia' esistente: _ORCHESTRATOR_AFFORDANCE_SEPARATOR). Aggiungere un test di accettazione che arma 2+ trigger simultaneamente e asserisce che stdout sia UN solo oggetto JSON parseabile che porta entrambi i contributi." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27, closed together with the twin bare-form row below (session-start-handler-two-emitters-still-use-broken-bare-additionalcontext-form) via the same accumulator refactor -- both name the same handler and the proposed fixes are one change. `handle_session_start` (src/des/adapters/drivers/hooks/session_start_handler.py) now accumulates `additional_context_parts: list[str]` + an optional `system_message` across the workflow-guidance, update-notice, gate-affordance-nudge, hook-version-skew, and orchestrator-affordance triggers, joining contributions with the existing `_ORCHESTRATOR_AFFORDANCE_SEPARATOR` and printing exactly ONE `json.dumps(...)` at the end of the function, always in the wrapped `hookSpecificOutput` form. `_emit_hook_version_skew_finding` was changed from a print-and-return-None side-effecting function to a pure `cwd -> str | None` classifier (still callable/monkeypatchable under its original name -- confirmed the sole other reference, tests/des/acceptance/classic_explicit_only/test_classic_removal_control_plane.py:493, only monkeypatches it to a no-op lambda, unaffected by the signature change). Deliberately left the substrate-probe advisory (`run_probe`, line ~830) UNTOUCHED -- it prints a plain one-line human-readable string, not JSON, and an existing test (tests/des/unit/adapters/drivers/hooks/test_session_start_handler.py::test_advisory_printed_to_stdout_when_probe_returns_non_empty) asserts on that exact raw-text contract; folding it into the JSON accumulator would have been unauthorized scope creep changing an already-tested, differently-shaped emitter. Removed the two now-dead trivial wrapper functions `_build_gate_affordance_output`/`_build_orchestrator_affordance_output` (no longer called, no test referenced them directly); kept `_build_update_output` since it has its own direct unit test and is still documented in the module docstring. New tests in tests/des/unit/adapters/drivers/hooks/test_session_start_handler.py::TestSessionStartHandlerSingleCombinedPayload (2 tests) arm 4 simultaneous triggers (workflow guidance + update-available + gate-affordance nudge + hook-version skew) and assert stdout is exactly ONE parseable JSON line carrying all four contributions plus systemMessage -- confirmed RED before the fix (4 separate stdout lines, `len(lines) == 1` failed) and GREEN after. One existing test had to be fixed as part of this change, and its own failure is direct proof the defect was real: tests/des/acceptance/classic_explicit_only/test_classic_removal_control_plane.py::test_real_session_start_emits_mode_guidance_without_prior_use_mutation extracted `additionalContext` from the BARE top-level form only (`payload.get('additionalContext', '')`) -- it was silently passing only because the workflow-guidance emitter used exactly that broken bare form; updated its extraction helper to read the wrapped `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` form (with a bare-form fallback kept only as a belt-and-suspenders regression guard). Verified: full run of session_start_handler's ~13 direct test files (Bugs 34/34, DES-acceptance 182/184, DES-integration 10/10, DES-unit 17/17) -- the 2 remaining acceptance failures in test_classic_removal_control_plane.py are PRE-EXISTING and unrelated (confirmed via `git stash`: same 2 cases already failed on HEAD before this change, over a missing literal 'migration' token in an unrelated guidance-message wording gap, not the print-consolidation defect); mypy clean on the touched src file; ruff + ruff format clean on all 3 touched files."
- [x] session-start-handler-two-emitters-still-use-broken-bare-additionalcontext-form: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Due dei sei emettitori in session_start_handler.py -- _emit_hook_version_skew_finding (riga 341) e la guida workflow-mode dentro handle_session_start (riga 797) -- usano ancora la forma print(json.dumps({\"additionalContext\": ...})) NUDA, che il docstring dello STESSO modulo (righe 19-21) dichiara esplicitamente rotta: 'The wrapped hookSpecificOutput form is required -- the bare additionalContext form is not honored by current Claude Code.' Non e' un'opinione di design: e' un fatto empiricamente stabilito dalla storia git dello stesso repo -- il commit a4a70fc99 ('fix(des): make SessionStart update notice visible and reliably injected') dichiara testualmente 'The handler emitted a bare additionalContext payload. That form is never shown to the user ... and is dropped by current Claude Code versions ... Verified visible end-to-end on Claude Code v2.1.177.' Quel fix ha riscritto SOLO l'emettitore della notifica update (ora correttamente incapsulato, righe 420-440/827) -- ma due emettitori aggiunti DOPO (skew M5, guida workflow-mode) hanno reintrodotto la stessa forma rotta. Confermato per lettura diretta: 3 dei 5 emettitori strutturati incapsulano correttamente (righe 420-440, 482-489, 531-538), 2 no. Costo: il finding di hook-version-skew e' esplicitamente documentato come 'il rilevatore PRIMARIO' per hook installati stantii che mancano l'applicazione U1/U2/U4 di atdd_pure -- un segnale di integrita' dell'enforcement -- e per il fatto gia' provato dal file stesso non raggiunge MAI il modello. Stesso per il nudge di migrazione workflow-mode-non-dichiarato. Entrambi sono codice morto dal punto di vista del modello: calcolati correttamente, gated correttamente, e persi all'ultima riga solo per il formato di wire. Nessun test cattura questo perche' nessuno asserisce sulla forma incapsulata-vs-nuda letterale consegnata a Claude Code -- controllano solo il contenuto della stringa a livello Python." proposed_solution="Instradare entrambi gli emettitori attraverso lo stesso helper hookSpecificOutput che gli altri tre gia' usano (idealmente l'accumulatore unico della riga gemella, cosi' c'e' UN solo punto di incapsulamento invece di 5 quasi-duplicati). Aggiungere un test di regressione che asserisce che nessun additionalContext raggiunga stdout non incapsulato -- questo chiude esattamente la classe di difetto che il commit a4a70fc99 ha gia' pagato per fissare una volta e che e' ricomparsa in silenzio." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27, same commit/change as the twin row above (session-start-handler-multiple-uncoordinated-prints-corrupt-stdout-json) -- see its resolution for the full mechanism. Both `_emit_hook_version_skew_finding` and the workflow-mode-guidance caller now feed the SAME single accumulator that the update-notice/gate-affordance/orchestrator-affordance emitters already fed correctly, so all five converge on ONE wrapped `hookSpecificOutput` encapsulation point instead of the prior 3-correct/2-bare split. New test tests/des/unit/adapters/drivers/hooks/test_session_start_handler.py::TestSessionStartHandlerSingleCombinedPayload::test_single_trigger_still_uses_wrapped_form_never_bare pins the skew finding specifically: asserts `'additionalContext' not in payload` at top level and that the finding text lives under `payload['hookSpecificOutput']['additionalContext']` instead -- confirmed RED before the fix (top-level bare key present) and GREEN after. Verified together with the twin row's full verification run."
- [x] step-completion-validator-mislabels-single-error-as-multiple-errors: paradigm=object-oriented defect="StepCompletionValidator._classify_error_type (src/des/domain/step_completion_validator.py:290-307) mislabella un caso di ERRORE SINGOLO come MULTIPLE_ERRORS. RIPRODOTTO DAL VIVO: quando lo status di una fase e' invalido (non in schema.valid_statuses), _validate_phase_event (righe 233-238) aggiunge SOLO a error_messages, non a missing_phases/incomplete_phases/invalid_skips. validate() imposta correttamente is_valid=False, ma _classify_error_type ispeziona solo le tre liste (tutte vuote) e cade nel default MULTIPLE_ERRORS -- mislabellando un singolo errore di 'status invalido'. Raggio attuale: NESSUNO -- il solo consumatore di produzione (subagent_stop_service.py) legge error_messages/recovery_suggestions ma mai error_type; gli unici lettori di .error_type sono i test unitari del validator stesso, che asseriscono direttamente la classificazione (bacata). Stessa classe della riga gemella sul detector Mandate-9-v2: logica di classificazione non banale, calcolata e testata in isolamento, senza nessun consumatore di produzione cablato -- output morto, silenziosamente sbagliato se mai un consumatore verra' aggiunto." proposed_solution="In _validate_phase_event, tracciare il caso 'status invalido' in una lista dedicata (o riusare incomplete_phases) invece di aggiungerlo solo a error_messages, cosi' _classify_error_type lo vede; aggiungere un valore error_type distinto (es. INVALID_STATUS) invece di cadere su MULTIPLE_ERRORS. Priorita' bassa da sistemare in isolamento dato che nulla lo consuma oggi, ma da notare prima che qualcuno cabli error_type in testo di rimedio user-facing (il proprio docstring del campo dice che esiste esattamente per quello)." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 exactly per proposed_solution. Confirmed real by direct read (RED verified empirically via git-stash: the existing test_validate_invalid_status test did not assert error_type at all, presumably because it was known-wrong). Added a dedicated `invalid_statuses: list[str]` field to CompletionResult and threaded it through validate()/_validate_phase_event(); _validate_phase_event's invalid-status branch now appends to it (in addition to the existing error_messages entry). _classify_error_type gained a fourth input and a new INVALID_STATUS branch (invalid_statuses non-empty, the other three empty), falling back to MULTIPLE_ERRORS only when 2+ of the four classes are non-empty. Extended the existing tests/des/unit/test_des_phase_event_value_objects.py::test_validate_invalid_status with a new sibling test asserting error_type == 'INVALID_STATUS' and invalid_statuses == ['RED_UNIT'] with the other three lists empty; RED-verified by stashing the src change and re-running (failed: 'MULTIPLE_ERRORS' == 'INVALID_STATUS'). No production consumer reads error_type today (confirmed via grep on subagent_stop_service.py), so this is purely fixing latent-wrong output before any consumer is wired to it. Verified: tests/des/unit/test_des_phase_event_value_objects.py (34/34) + tests/des/unit/application/test_subagent_stop_service.py (4/4) green; mypy clean on src/des/domain/step_completion_validator.py."
- [x] g-distill-pre-short-circuit-skips-clear-on-allow-leaks-wave-entering-flag: paradigm=object-oriented defect="_evaluate_distill_dispatch_gate (G-DISTILL-PRE, src/des/adapters/drivers/hooks/pre_tool_use_handler.py:489-504) gira PRIMA di _peek_wave_entering/activation.clear_entry (righe 526-577). Su un set completo di marker D_DISTILL ritorna ('allow', None) e handle_pre_tool_use fa immediatamente exit_code=0; return -- saltando il peek dell'entrata wave, l'intercetto carpaccio U1, e la scrittura clear-on-allow del tutto. clear_entry viene chiamato da UN SOLO punto in tutto il codebase (riga 575), quindi nulla altro pulisce il flag. RIPRODOTTO DAL VIVO: armato un WaveActiveRecord(wave='distill', provenance=COMMAND, entry_pending=True) (esattamente cio' che l'ancora UserPromptSubmit scrive su /nw-distill), passato un prompt D_DISTILL ben formato alla _evaluate_distill_dispatch_gate reale -- entry_pending resta True dopo il dispatch. Viola l'invariante I4 di wave_active.py:43-46 ('entry_pending=True e' scritto SOLO dal COMMAND arm... il gate side legge e (post-allow) CANCELLA') e il contratto composition-seat dichiarato dallo stesso handler (righe 520-525, 'clear-on-allow NORMATIVE'). Conseguenza: wave_entering resta True e viene consumato dal PROSSIMO dispatch Agent/Task qualsiasi (non necessariamente legato a DISTILL) in pre_tool_use_service.py:214-229, esentandolo dalla cascata DENY per marker DES-VALIDATION mancante anche se non e' davvero il dispatch entrante-in-wave. La finestra si chiude al dispatch successivo (che chiama clear_entry normalmente), ma dato che il JIT DISTILL per-slice e' canonico in atdd_pure, questo scatta a OGNI chiamata DISTILL di ogni slice." proposed_solution="Spostare il return del ramo distill_verdict=='allow' DOPO la sequenza peek/clear dell'entrata wave (o chiamare esplicitamente _peek_wave_entering + activation.clear_entry prima di ritornare in quel ramo), cosi' G-DISTILL-PRE corto-circuita SOLO la chiamata al servizio di validazione template classico (il suo intento dichiarato, righe 492-495), non il ciclo di vita dell'entrata che ogni altro percorso ALLOW onora." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 exactly per proposed_solution (second variant): the distill_verdict=='allow' branch in handle_pre_tool_use (src/des/adapters/drivers/hooks/pre_tool_use_handler.py) now explicitly calls _peek_wave_entering + activation.clear_entry before returning, instead of returning immediately -- mirroring the same peek/clear-on-allow sequence every other ALLOW path runs (lines ~526-577), without invoking the classic template-validation service (its declared short-circuit intent). New tests/bugs/des/test_distill_pre_allow_clears_entry_pending.py arms a WaveActiveRecord(wave='distill', entry_pending=True) floor, drives a well-formed D_DISTILL prompt through the real handle_pre_tool_use() hook entrypoint, and confirms entry_pending is False afterward (RED verified before the fix: entry_pending stayed True; GREEN after) plus a companion no-pending-entry case. - [x] post-tool-use-handler-naive-des-task-substring-bypasses-canonical-marker-parser: paradigm=object-oriented defect="post_tool_use_handler.py:105 decide is_des_task = 'DES-VALIDATION' in prompt -- un semplice controllo di sottostringa, senza mai importare/chiamare DesMarkerParser (src/des/domain/des_marker_parser.py), il parser canonico e puro-dominio che il resto del codebase usa proprio per distinguere un marker VALIDATO da una menzione in prosa. Viola GDP-8 (decidi sulla PROPRIETA', mai sulla DESIGNAZIONE) e il principio SSOT/DRY (la logica di parsing dei marker DES appartiene al domain layer, non va reimplementata ad-hoc in un adapter driver). Il fratello subagent_stop_handler.py:208 ha un controllo simile MA solo come pre-filtro economico, seguito immediatamente da markers = DesMarkerParser().parse(content); if not markers.is_des_task: continue -- la decisione REALE e' delegata la' al parser canonico. post_tool_use_handler.py e' l'unico dei fratelli che non chiama MAI il parser. Conseguenza: is_des_task alimenta PostToolUseService.check_completion_status (post_tool_use_service.py:41) -- un dispatch NON-DES qualsiasi (es. DISCUSS/DESIGN/ricerca) il cui prompt menziona 'DES-VALIDATION' in prosa viene misclassificato e l'orchestratore riceve la guida fuorviante 'Continue the DELIVER workflow. Dispatch the next step.' iniettata come se fosse autorevole." proposed_solution="Importare DesMarkerParser (gia' dipendenza stabile, pura, senza I/O, usata da un fratello) e sostituire la riga 105 con is_des_task = DesMarkerParser().parse(prompt).is_des_task, rispecchiando l'uso di subagent_stop_handler.py. - [x] audit-events-category-helpers-are-dead-and-decide-on-a-name-prefix: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`get_event_category` and `validate_event_type` (src/des/adapters/driven/logging/audit_events.py:153-189) have ZERO production callers -- grep over src/ scripts/ lib/ finds only their own definitions plus one test module, tests/des/unit/adapters/driven/logging/test_audit_events_hook_types.py, which imports them by module-attribute lookup purely to exercise them. Dead production code whose only consumer is the test written to test it. Additionally `get_event_category` is a GDP-8 violation in itself: it derives an event's category by chaining `event_type.startswith(...)` over seven literal prefixes -- deciding on the DESIGNATION (how the enum member happens to be spelled) rather than on the property, when `EventType` is an Enum that could carry its category as data. A newly added member whose name does not start with one of the seven literals silently becomes the string 'UNKNOWN' with no signal, and the prefixes are order-sensitive by construction (any future member prefixed by an existing one is captured by the earlier branch)." proposed_solution="Delete both functions and their dedicated test module unless a production consumer is identified; if the category concept is genuinely needed, attach it to `EventType` as an enum-level attribute so the category is DATA carried by the member, not re-derived from how its name is spelled." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 exactly per proposed_solution, first branch (delete). Checked whether the dedicated test module also covered anything else load-bearing: it also asserted the 4 HOOK EventType enum members exist, but that property is independently covered by tests/des/unit/application/test_orchestrator_validate_prompt_audit.py (which exercises HOOK_PRE_TASK_PASSED/HOOK_PRE_TASK_BLOCKED on the real validate_prompt production path), so deleting the module drops no unique coverage. Deleted both functions from audit_events.py and removed tests/des/unit/adapters/driven/logging/test_audit_events_hook_types.py in full. Did not attach a category to EventType-as-data (second branch) since no production consumer ever needed the category concept in the first place. Verified: tests/des/unit/adapters/driven/logging/ (48/48) + test_orchestrator_validate_prompt_audit.py green; mypy clean on src/des/adapters/driven/logging/audit_events.py; repo-wide grep for both deleted names now empty."

- [x] tdd-phase-validator-treats-substring-of-english-word-as-phase-present: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`TDDPhaseValidator._is_phase_present_in_prompt` (src/des/application/validator.py:220-231, wired via TemplateValidator into orchestrator.py:96 and service_factory.py:76, i.e. live on the classic dispatch path) filters prompt lines with a bare substring test `if phase in line` before falling into `_is_missing_context`, which itself correctly uses word-boundary regex (`\b{phase}\b`). Because the outer filter has no word boundary, any line containing an English word that merely CONTAINS the phase as a substring (CREDENTIALS/CREDIT/PREDICATE/SHREDDED all contain RED) satisfies `phase in line`, and since `_is_missing_context`'s own `\bRED\b` checks do not match inside those words either, the line is not classified as missing-context and the phase is reported PRESENT. REPRODUCED by direct execution: `TDDPhaseValidator().validate('...use your CREDENTIALS to access the API. Then GREEN light the deploy and COMMIT the change...')` returns `[]` (no errors) even though the RED phase is never mentioned as an actual TDD phase word -- the CREDENTIALS substring alone satisfies it. Same GDP-8 designation-vs-property class already caught repeatedly elsewhere in this tree (axis_b_levers.py import substring, feature_classifier.py heading substring, feature_delta_schema.py composite substring), but a distinct file/method not covered by those rows." proposed_solution="Apply the same `\b{phase}\b` word-boundary regex already used inside `_is_missing_context` to the OUTER filter in `_is_phase_present_in_prompt`, so a line only enters candidacy when the phase actually appears as a whole word; add a regression test asserting a prompt containing only CREDENTIALS/CREDIT-like words (and no whole-word RED) reports the RED phase as missing." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27. Confirmed the exact reproduction in the row (CREDENTIALS/GREEN/COMMIT prompt returns [] with RED never reported missing) via a new RED test, tests/installer/unit/test_template_validator.py::test_phase_substring_inside_unrelated_word_is_not_present. A NAIVE plain `\b{phase}\b` fix (no legacy suffix) broke 3 pre-existing green tests: test_all_tdd_phases_present and the SSOT-driven tests in test_validator_schema_versioning.py/test_des_template_validator.py all construct prompts using the LEGACY 5-phase spellings RED_ACCEPTANCE/RED_UNIT and expect them to satisfy the canonical 3-phase (RED/GREEN/COMMIT) check -- this is INTENTIONAL per the class docstring ('legacy 5-phase contract... remains accepted for replay'), not incidental. Applied the narrower pattern `\b{phase}(?:_\w+)?\b`: word-boundary-anchored on both sides, with an optional `_SUFFIX` extension so RED_ACCEPTANCE/RED_UNIT still count as RED, while CREDENTIALS/CREDIT/PREDICATE/SHREDDED (RED embedded directly in a run of letters, no boundary) do not. Verified: tests/installer/unit/test_template_validator.py + tests/des/unit/application/test_validator_dual_canon.py + tests/des/unit/test_validator_schema_versioning.py + tests/installer/unit/test_des_template_validator.py + tests/des/acceptance/test_execute_template_sync.py all green, 64/64. mypy clean on src/des/application/validator.py."

Proof the risk was already known: `scripts/release/publish_experimental.py:60-132`'s own RSYNC_FILTER claims (comment, lines 60-63) to mirror release-prod.yml's block 'verbatim', yet it alone adds `--exclude=.claude/`, `--exclude=CLAUDE.md`, `--exclude=ARCH_TECH_DEBT.md`, `--exclude=.mailmap`, `--exclude=.tla-swarm-model/`, `--exclude=.test_durations` -- meaning someone already recognized these five/six paths must not ship, wired the fix into the ONE channel that happens not to be the real prod/RC pipelines, and never back-ported it. Separately, release-rc.yml's filter also lacks the `--include=.gitignore` fix release-prod.yml's own inline comment (lines 844-856) says was added after a dated 2026-07-22 incident where a stale target-side .gitignore silently swallowed real source packages -- a second instance of the same three-copies-drift mechanism, not a second bug." proposed_solution="Stop hand-maintaining the rsync filter block three times (prod workflow, rc workflow, publish_experimental.py). Extract it to one shared, versioned source (e.g. a checked-in rsync-filter file all three invoke with `--filter=merge <path>`, or a small Python helper that both workflows and publish_experimental.py call) so a fix or an added exclude applies everywhere by construction. Immediately add the missing `--exclude 'CLAUDE.md'`, `--exclude 'ARCH_TECH_DEBT.md'`, `--exclude '.mailmap'`, `--exclude '.claude/'`, `--exclude '.tla-swarm-model/'` to release-prod.yml and release-rc.yml, and the missing `--include '.gitignore'` ordering fix to release-rc.yml. Add an AT that runs the real prod rsync filter (or a stand-in exercising the same exclude list) against a fixture tree containing these five paths and asserts none survive." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27. Confirmed via read: release-prod.yml:842-912 and release-rc.yml:1095-1141 never excluded CLAUDE.md / ARCH_TECH_DEBT.md / .mailmap / .claude/ / .tla-swarm-model/, while publish_experimental.py RSYNC_FILTER already did (lines ~128-133). - [x] wheel-no-src-imports-check-decides-on-a-line-substring-not-a-parsed-import: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`_check_no_src_imports` (scripts/validation/validate_installed_wheel.py:192-216) verifies the installed wheel never imports the pre-rewrite `src.des` path by scanning every line of every installed .py file for the raw substrings `from src.des` / `import src.des`, skipping only lines whose STRIPPED text starts with `#`. It does not parse the file and does not recognize multi-line docstrings/comments-inside-strings, so a line INSIDE a triple-quoted docstring that merely QUOTES or DISCUSSES the banned import (e.g. a migration-note docstring line reading `We used to import from src.des directly; now we do not.`) does not start with `#` and trips a false FAIL, reproduced by direct execution of the same per-line loop against that exact string. This is the same GDP-8 designation-vs-property shape caught repeatedly elsewhere in this pile (axis_b_levers.py import substring, feature_delta_schema.py heading substring): deciding an import fact by scanning raw text for a phrase instead of consulting the file's actual AST import nodes. Lower severity than the other instances because the true-positive path (an actual `from src.des import x` line) is still caught correctly and the failure mode is fail-CLOSED (a false FAIL blocks a release build loudly, not a silent pass) -- but it is still a spurious, non-reproducible-by-inspection release blocker waiting for the day a docstring mentions the banned import path in prose." proposed_solution="Parse each installed .py file with `ast.parse` and walk `ast.Import`/`ast.ImportFrom` nodes checking the dotted module head against `src.des`, instead of a raw per-line substring scan; degrade to a named INDETERMINATE (not a silent pass) for any file that fails to parse, consistent with the CodeFactPort/AST pattern already used elsewhere in the tree (src/des/adapters/driven/codefact/ast_code_fact_adapter.py)." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27. Rewrote check_no_src_imports (function is public, not the underscore-prefixed name the row cited -- same file/lines, name drifted since the row was filed) to ast.parse each file and walk real ast.Import/ast.ImportFrom nodes checking the dotted module name against src.des, replacing the raw per-line substring scan. Did NOT add a third INDETERMINATE CheckResult state (that dataclass has only passed: bool and is consumed by run_install_smoke/callers repo-wide -- widening it is a real design change, out of scope for a mechanical drain fix); instead a file that fails to parse is reported as a FAILURE citing the file + parse error, fail-loud rather than silently skipped, which is the property the proposed_solution's degrade-LOUD intent actually needs even without a new enum member. RED-verified first: new tests/build/unit/test_check_no_src_imports_ast_based.py (6 fast in-process tests against a synthetic site-packages tree, no wheel build) -- 2 of 6 failed pre-fix (docstring false-positive + silently-skipped-unparseable-file, verified via git stash), confirming the row claim by execution; all 6 green post-fix, plus a true-positive pair (real import/from-import) pinned so the fix cannot regress into a false negative. Also ran the slow real-wheel integration suite tests/build/unit/test_install_smoke.py (marked slow, session-shared wheel) directly -- 3/3 green, confirming the rewrite still passes end-to-end against an actual built wheel. mypy clean on scripts/validation/validate_installed_wheel.py (not under src/des/, so the strict gate does not formally apply, but ran it anyway); ruff clean on both touched files."

Consider a cheap drift gate: a script that greps nw-product-owner.md for the DoR Checklist heading and diffs its 9 bullet lines against dor-items.yaml items, failing loud on any divergence -- the same self-verifying-citation pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase's catalog-coherence checks." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27. Blast radius was WIDER than the row's single-file framing -- the same short-form item-9 string is a pinned literal in the dor_items_ssot acceptance suite (2 feature files + 4 step/domain-type files) AND in the actual loaded skill nw-dor-validation/SKILL.md's Item-9 heading (the enforcement path the reviewer follows), so all were synced together to keep the SSOT and its consumers textually identical rather than leaving a half-migrated wording. Did NOT build the proposed drift gate (comparing dor-items.yaml against nw-product-owner.md mechanically) -- noted as a follow-up, out of scope for a mechanical drain item; check_dor_items_drift.py already exists but only compares item COUNT, not exact wording. - [x] agent-tools-frontmatter-substring-membership-check-not-exact: paradigm=object-oriented defect="scripts/validation/validate_framework_templates.py's A12 rule (reviewer-must-not-have-write-tools, lines 227-235) reads fm.get('tools', '') then does: for forbidden in ('Write', 'Edit'): if forbidden in tools. For every agent file in nWave/agents/ except one, the frontmatter tools: line is written as a bare comma-separated scalar (e.g. tools: Read, Glob, Grep, Task, Bash, mcp__tsunami__callers_of, ...) with no YAML brackets, so yaml.safe_load parses it as a single STRING, not a list -- confirmed by direct execution (yaml.safe_load of a bare tools: line returns a str). A12's if forbidden in tools is therefore a raw SUBSTRING search over that string, not a check against a tokenized set of granted tool names -- the exact GDP-8 designation-vs-property shape (deciding a structured fact via in-on-text instead of parsing the actual tokens). It has not yet produced a wrong verdict only because no current reviewer's tool string happens to contain the literal substrings Write or Edit embedded inside a longer, legitimately-granted token (e.g. a future rewrite-named or edit-named mcp tool would trip a false A12 failure on a reviewer never granted Write/Edit at all). Aggravating inconsistency confirmed in the same directory: nWave/agents/nw-documentarist-reviewer.md line 6 is the ONE file that writes tools with YAML flow-list brackets, which parses to an actual Python list -- so the SAME line of validator code evaluates a list-membership check for that one file and a substring-of-string check for all 50 others, an unintentional behavioral fork driven purely by which YAML scalar style an author happened to use." proposed_solution="Tokenize the tools value before the A12 check regardless of whether frontmatter parsed it as str or list -- split on comma and strip when it is a string, use the list as-is otherwise -- then check exact membership of the forbidden name in that token set instead of substring-in-string. Optionally also normalize all tools frontmatter lines to one YAML style (bracket or bare) repo-wide so the field's parsed type stops depending on author style, matching scripts/docgen.py's already-defensive str-or-list handling of the same ambiguity." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 (techdebt drain shard P). RED-verified first: added 2 new tests to tests/build/unit/test_a12_reviewer_bash_allowed.py -- a reviewer granted a synthetic tool mcp__wiki__SuggestEdit (both as a bare comma-scalar string and as a bracketed YAML list) -- both failed pre-fix via git stash (A12 wrongly fired on the Edit substring), confirming the row claim by execution. Fix: A12 now tokenizes the tools value (split-on-comma-and-strip when str, use-as-is when already a list -- mirroring scripts/docgen.py extract_agent, exactly as proposed_solution suggested) before checking membership, replacing the raw substring-in-string check. Verified: 6/6 green in the targeted test file post-fix; ran the real validator against the actual nWave/agents/ tree (scripts/validation/validate_framework_templates.py) and confirmed zero A12 findings both before and after -- the fix does not change any current real-agent verdict, only closes the future false-positive window the row named. Did NOT do the optional second half of proposed_solution (normalizing all tools frontmatter to one YAML style repo-wide) -- unnecessary now that both styles parse to the same token set, and out of scope for a mechanical drain fix. ruff check + ruff format clean on both touched files; not under src/des/, so mypy strict does not formally apply."
- [x] utility-scripts-plugin-list-parity-still-untested-row-closed-on-a-different-pair: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The whitelist of distributed utility scripts is declared in THREE places, not two: scripts/build_dist.py:68-72 `UTILITY_SCRIPTS`, scripts/install/plugins/utilities_plugin.py:40-44 `UtilitiesPlugin.UTILITY_SCRIPTS`, and the wheel force-include block. defects.md:122 (utility-scripts-whitelist-restated-in-two-places) named the build_dist <-> utilities_plugin pair and asked for a test asserting their equality; it was CLOSED 2026-07-26 citing tests/release/test_wheel_utility_scripts_invariant.py -- but that module imports only `build_dist.UTILITY_SCRIPTS` (line 50) and compares it against the WHEEL FORCE-INCLUDE map (test_utility_script_is_force_included, test_no_utility_script_silently_dropped). It never mentions utilities_plugin. grep across tests/ confirms NO test compares the two UTILITY_SCRIPTS constants. The pair that row named is still unchecked, so a script added to build_dist but not to the plugin still reaches dist/ and never reaches the target -- exactly the silent partial failure the row described. The closure is itself a GDP-8 instance: the row was retired on a DESIGNATION (a test whose name matches 'utility scripts invariant' exists) instead of the PROPERTY (the specific pair is compared)." proposed_solution="Have UtilitiesPlugin import UTILITY_SCRIPTS from the one declaring module instead of restating it, collapsing three declarations to one; failing that, add the two-constant equality test the original row asked for and reopen/annotate defects.md:122 so its resolution names what it actually verified." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 (techdebt drain shard P). RE-VERIFIED THE PROPOSED_SOLUTION FIRST AND FOUND IT UNSAFE: the primary option -- have UtilitiesPlugin import UTILITY_SCRIPTS from build_dist -- would break real installs, because scripts/build_dist.py is a dev/release-only script never force-included into the nwave-ai wheel (pyproject.toml packages map only ships scripts/install and scripts/shared); UtilitiesPlugin runs from the INSTALLED wheel on a user machine, where that import would raise ModuleNotFoundError. Took the documented fallback instead: added TestBuildDistAndUtilitiesPluginAgree.test_utility_scripts_lists_are_identical to the existing tests/release/test_wheel_utility_scripts_invariant.py, asserting build_dist.UTILITY_SCRIPTS == UtilitiesPlugin.UTILITY_SCRIPTS (dev-repo test-time only, no runtime cross-import). Confirmed the two lists are IDENTICAL today (both: install_nwave_target_hooks.py, refactor_agent.py, validate_step_file.py) -- so this closes the missing-guard gap the row named without any drift to reconcile. RED-verified by mutation: temporarily added a 4th entry to UtilitiesPlugin.UTILITY_SCRIPTS -- new test failed with the drift named explicitly; reverted, test green. 5/5 in the targeted test module post-fix. Did NOT reopen/annotate defects.md:122 (out of scope for this drain, that ledger is a separate file); this row itself documents the correction. ruff check + ruff format clean; not under src/des/, so mypy strict does not formally apply."
- [x] suite-covers-production-lines-decides-on-an-import-substring-not-an-import: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`_suite_covers_production_lines` (src/des/cli/axis_b_levers.py:1069-1087) decides whether the driven workspace's ATs reach production code with `if 'import des' in text or 'from des' in text` over the raw file text. It is a designation-check, not a property-check, and is wrong in both directions. FALSE POSITIVE, proven by execution: `'import des' in 'import destroyer'` is True and `'from des' in 'from descriptors import x'` is True -- any module whose name merely STARTS with 'des' satisfies the lever, as does the string appearing inside a comment, a docstring, or a string literal (including a comment saying there is no `import des` here). FALSE NEGATIVE: `import des.application.foo as f` under an aliasing style the substring misses, or a conditional/`importlib` import. The lever's remediation text asserts a strong claim -- 'the contract suite drives zero src/des production lines (coverage theater)' -- on evidence that cannot support it. Violates GDP-8 (key on the property the object HAS -- it imports `des` -- never on a string pattern that stands for it) and GDP-7's execution-observing clause (the docstring itself says 'pure text read'). The tree already owns the right instrument: src/des/adapters/driven/codefact/ast_code_fact_adapter.py parses imports properly, per ADR-LA-001 (CodeFactPort). Distinct from the closed defects row axis-b-levers-hardcode-nwave-paths, which was about the hardcoded tests/ and src/des/ path layout, not about how an import is recognized." proposed_solution="Resolve the import fact through the CodeFactPort/AST adapter (ast.parse -> ast.Import / ast.ImportFrom with a module-name check on the dotted head) instead of a substring over file text, and degrade to Indeterminate rather than False when a file cannot be parsed -- today a syntax error is swallowed by the `continue` and reads as 'no production coverage'." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 (techdebt drain shard P). Rewrote _suite_covers_production_lines to ast.parse each tests/test_*.py file and check real ast.Import/ast.ImportFrom nodes dotted-module head == des, replacing the raw substring scan. DEVIATED from proposed_solution on ONE point, with reason: did NOT route through the existing CodeFactPort/ast_code_fact_adapter.py -- re-verified that adapter first and found it answers graph queries (callers-of/reads-of/never-wired) over CodeFactChain, which is built from THIS repos own src/des tree; the lever scans an ARBITRARY target workspaces tests/ directory (the repo argument), which is not something CodeFactChain indexes. A direct ast.parse over the target files own text is the right-shaped tool here, not a mismatched port. Added the GDP-6 indeterminate branch the row asked for: a file that fails to parse is now reported via a new health.gate.coverage-on-executed-path.parse-error.indeterminate structured_event (flagged=False, naming the file(s)) instead of being silently continue-d past as if it had no production import -- mirrors the existing check_undefined_name NOT_APPLICABLE convention already in this same module. RED-verified first via git stash: 3 of 4 new tests failed on the pre-fix substring scan (module-named-like-des false positive, docstring-quoting false positive, unparseable-file false-theater); the 4th (aliased real import) happened to pass by substring-coincidence pre-fix too, kept as a true-positive guard against a future regression. All 12 tests in tests/des/unit/cli/test_coverage_on_executed_path_runner_aware.py green post-fix (8 pre-existing + 4 new), including the 4 pre-existing tests proving zero behavior change on the already-covered runner-aware/theater/real-coverage paths. mypy clean on src/des/cli/axis_b_levers.py; ruff check + ruff format clean on both touched files."
- [x] five-of-seven-polyglot-runners-shell-out-with-no-timeout-bound: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Of the seven language runner adapters in `src/des/adapters/driven/runner/`, only two -- `pytest_runner.py` (`run_pytest_scope`, timeout=run_timeout_seconds()) and `cargo_runner.py` (`run_cargo_scope`/`list_cargo_scope`, same helper, with explicit `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` handling that raises a named `RunnerAdapterUnavailable`) -- bound their `subprocess.run` call with a timeout. The other five -- `go_runner.py:89` (`run_go_scope`), `java_runner.py:106` (`run_java_scope`), `kotlin_runner.py:100`, `vitest_runner.py:85`, `csharp_runner.py:104` -- call `subprocess.run([resolution.path, *subcommand], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=target_root, env=...)` with no `timeout=` argument at all (grepped every file in the directory for `timeout=`; only the two named above have it). All five already import and call the shared `_signal_kill_reason` helper from `pytest_runner.py` to translate an OS-killed process into a named INDETERMINATE, so the intent to degrade LOUD on abnormal termination is present -- it simply never fires for a HUNG (not killed) target-language test run, which instead blocks the calling DES gate/service forever, same failure shape as the already-filed git-adapters-subprocess-calls-have-no-timeout row but in the polyglot test-execution seam instead of the git seam. The five files are near-identical copy-paste siblings (same resolve_tool -> subprocess.run -> _signal_kill_reason shape, differing only in binary name and known-install-locations), which is itself the SSOT/DRY gap that let the timeout omission propagate to all five instead of being caught once in a shared base." proposed_solution="Add `timeout=run_timeout_seconds()` (the same helper cargo/pytest already import from pytest_runner.py) to each of the five `subprocess.run` calls, and wrap each in the same `except subprocess.TimeoutExpired` -> `RunnerAdapterUnavailable` translation cargo_runner.py already demonstrates, OR -- better, since the five are structurally identical -- extract a single shared `_run_shelled_scope(adapter, resolved_binary, subcommand, target_root, env)` helper in a common module that all five call, so the timeout bound and TimeoutExpired handling are enforced once instead of needing five independent edits (and future language adapters inherit the bound by construction instead of by memory)." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 (techdebt drain shard Q). RED test first: tests/des/unit/adapters/driven/runner/test_polyglot_runners_timeout_bound.py (new, parametrized over all 5) mocked subprocess.run to raise TimeoutExpired and confirmed the raw exception propagated uncaught pre-fix (5/5 failed for the right reason). GREEN: added `timeout=run_timeout_seconds()` (the shared helper cargo/pytest already import from pytest_runner.py) to the subprocess.run call in go_runner.py, java_runner.py, kotlin_runner.py, vitest_runner.py, csharp_runner.py, each wrapped in try/except subprocess.TimeoutExpired -> RunnerAdapterUnavailable, mirroring cargo_runner.py verbatim (mechanical option from proposed_solution, not the shared-helper-extraction option -- five near-identical 15-line edits was lower blast radius than a new abstraction for a techdebt-drain mandate). Verified: new test 5/5 passed; full acceptance suites for all 5 runners (go/java/kotlin/vitest/csharp_test_runner_adapter, 27 tests) green, no regression; mypy clean on all 5 touched src files."
- [x] utilities-plugin-verify-passes-on-any-py-file-in-a-shared-directory: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`UtilitiesPlugin.verify()` (scripts/install/plugins/utilities_plugin.py:201-249) declares success when `list(target_scripts_dir.glob('*.py'))` is non-empty -- it never checks that any of the three scripts it is responsible for actually landed. The directory it globs is `context.claude_dir / 'scripts'`, which the plugin's OWN module docstring states is SHARED with the DES family (confirmed: des_plugin.py:1199, :1270, :2114, :2252 all write into `context.claude_dir / 'scripts'`). So the DES plugin's files alone satisfy this verification even when ZERO utility scripts were installed. Compounding it, `install()` (line 103) does `if not source_script.exists(): continue` -- a script missing from the dist payload is skipped in silence and install still returns success=True. Net effect: a payload that ships none of install_nwave_target_hooks.py / refactor_agent.py / validate_step_file.py installs green and verifies green. Violates GDP-8 (decide on the property 'each declared utility script is present at the target', never on the proxy designation 'some file with a .py extension exists in a shared directory') and GDP-6 (silent-wrong rather than degrade-LOUD). This is the mechanism behind the already-filed refactor-actuator-shipped-nowhere defect (defects.md:120) -- that row records the SYMPTOM on a user machine; this row is the check that should have caught it and structurally cannot." proposed_solution="Make verify() assert the property: for each name in UTILITY_SCRIPTS, the target file exists (and, matching install()'s own version logic, is at the shipped version), failing with the specific missing names. Make install() FAIL rather than `continue` when a declared source script is absent from the payload -- an absent script is a broken build, not an optional item." resolution="Fixed 2026-07-27 (techdebt drain shard Q), SCOPED to the verify()-side of the row (its own title/GDP-8 claim). RED test first: tests/plugins/plugin-architecture/unit/test_utilities_plugin_verify_property.py -- a target directory holding only an unrelated .py file (standing in for a DES-family neighbour) passed verify() pre-fix. GREEN: verify() now checks each name in UTILITY_SCRIPTS for presence at the target instead of a bare glob('*.py') count, failing with the specific missing names (two failure shapes: all-missing keeps the old 'no utility scripts found' substring for backward-compat, partial-missing names exactly which declared scripts are absent). Found and fixed a LATENT gap this exposed: the existing utility_scripts_source test fixture in test_utilities_plugin.py shipped only 2 of the 3 declared UTILITY_SCRIPTS (missing refactor_agent.py) and several tests asserted verify() success against that incomplete fixture -- updated the fixture to ship all three, matching a real dist payload. DID NOT touch the install()-side half of proposed_solution (making install() FAIL rather than continue when a source script is absent from the payload): re-verified first and found real existing test coverage relies on the current skip-silently behavior with an intentionally incomplete source fixture (test_utilities_plugin_install_success_with_mock_source and others), so a stricter install() is a wider, more consequential behavior change (a build that legitimately ships a staged/partial payload would now hard-fail) needing its own reasoned pass, not a byproduct of the verify() fix -- left for a follow-up, the verify()-side fix already closes the row title GDP-8 claim (checking a proxy designation instead of the property) on its own. Verified: 190/190 across tests/plugins/plugin-architecture/, tests/installer/acceptance/test_install_cleanup_stale.py, tests/plugins/install/test_wrapper_plugins.py, tests/installer/unit/plugins/test_runtime_asset_family_manifests.py, and tests/release/test_wheel_utility_scripts_invariant.py -- no regression. Not under src/des/, so mypy strict does not formally apply; ruff clean on all touched files."
- [x] claude-md-ci-table-stale-vs-ci-yml-job-count-and-matrix: paradigm=object-oriented defect="CLAUDE.md's CI/CD Pipeline section describes .github/workflows/ci.yml as a 4-stage pipeline -- '1. Fast checks: commitlint, code-quality, file-quality, security (~1 min)', '2. Framework validation: catalog schema, version consistency, docs freshness (~1 min)', '3. Cross-platform tests: Ubuntu x Python 3.11/3.12 matrix (~10 min)', '4. Agent sync (~1 min)'. The real ci.yml (1515 lines) defines 20 top-level jobs, not a 4-row summary of them: contamination-guard, classify-dependabot, commitlint, commit-author-validation, code-quality, file-quality, documentation-links, feature-delta-token-budget, security-checks, probe-method-check, port-realization-gate, framework-validation, spine-formal-verify, rule-maturity-consistency, plan, test, coverage-combine, agent-sync, notify-slack, ci-success. The test matrix itself (the plan job, lines 710-733) no longer matches '3.11/3.12' either: per-push/PR CI now runs ONLY Python 3.12 (a single version) crossed with a git_topology axis (main_checkout/worktree) and 4 shards (8 jobs total), while the twice-weekly schedule run and workflow_dispatch instead sweep 3.10, 3.11, 3.12, 3.13, 3.14 x 2 topologies x 4 shards (40 jobs). Re-verified the claim against the live .github/workflows/ci.yml before editing: confirmed 20 top-level jobs (grepped job-definition lines) and confirmed the plan job (lines 710-733) selects python-version 3.12-only x 2 git_topology x 4 shard (8 jobs) on push/PR vs 3.10-3.14 x 2 x 4 (40 jobs) on schedule/workflow_dispatch, exactly as the row states. Took the row own second option (pointer-to-job-names instead of a static per-row table, since 20 jobs across a dynamic push-vs-schedule matrix resist a 4-row summary that will only go stale again): rewrote the CI/CD Pipeline > CI section in the repo-root CLAUDE.md to name the real job count and rough groups, and to state the git_topology + shard + push-vs-schedule split explicitly. Did NOT touch the private global ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md, which carries the same stale table -- that file lives outside this git repo and outside this worktree, not something a repo commit can carry (same scope call as the prior claude-md-project-structure-cites-23-agents closure). Also did NOT touch the adjacent Release section, which names a nonexistent .github/workflows/release.yml (the real files are release-dev.yml/release-rc.yml/release-prod.yml/yank-release.yml) -- a distinct staleness this row did not name, out of scope for this drain pass, flagged here for whoever picks it up next. No test pins the old table text (grepped for it, zero hits) so no test changes needed; doc-only, mypy/tests N/A."
- [x] event-name-constants-split-port-adapter: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Feature-end event-name constants are defined in src/des/ports/driven_ports/at_completion_ledger_port.py:38-39 (ENVIRONMENTAL_E2E_GATE_RAN, ENVIRONMENTAL_E2E_VERIFIED) and manually re-exported by src/des/adapters/driven/logging/at_completion_ledger.py:63,105-109, creating a ripple: new event names must be defined in the port first, then manually re-imported/re-exported by the adapter.
